\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{a_{h}}+||\partial_{t}^{a}\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

(5.44)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}+1}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

with
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and
k_{g}\geq 2
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
and with
c
independent of
h
.

If furthermore we assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
(that is
V_{h}=\Lambda_{h}
) we obtain the following improved estimates

(5.45)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{a_{h}}+||\partial_{t}^{a}\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

(5.46)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{k_{g}-1})||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

(5.47)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

(5.48)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+h^{1/2}||\partial_{t}^{a}\big(({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\big)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

with
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
and with constant
c
independent of
h
.

Proof.

We focus on the case where
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, since we will see that the general case follows in a similar way. Let us start with (
a=0
). First, let us consider
\mathbf{u}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
to be the finite element solution of the following surface Stokes problem:

(5.49)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})\ +b^{L}({\mathbf{v}},\{p,\lambda\})

\displaystyle=({\mathbf{g}},{\mathbf{v}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma),

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{u}},\{q,\xi\})

\displaystyle=0\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{q,\xi\}\in(L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}(\Gamma)),

with
{\mathbf{g}}={\bm{f}}-\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}-\nabla_{\Gamma}p-\lambda{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}
and
{\mathbf{g}}_{h}={\mathbf{g}}^{-\ell}
as in
[
17
]
. Notice that since
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
is the solution to (
3.6
),
({\mathbf{u}},\{0,0\})
is the solution of (
5.49
).

Then, by
[
17
, Theorem 6.13, Theorem 6.14, Corollary 6.1]
we readily see, with the help of known consistency errors, that the following error estimates hold,

(5.50)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)},

(5.51)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)},

with
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
and
c=c_{1}(||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}+||p||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\lambda||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)})
. We are now able to prove the required estimates. Let us start with the energy estimate of (
5.45
). Combining the above estimate (
5.50
), the pseudo Galerkin orthogonality (
5.42
) and the improved
H^{1}
coercivity estimate (
5.24
) we obtain, since
\mathbf{u}_{h}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
,

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle=a_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h})+a_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{u}_{h}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))

\displaystyle\leq||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\big(||\mathbf{u}_{h}-{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}+ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

The energy bound in (
5.45
) is then obtained after a simple kickback argument. The pressure bound in (
5.45
) can be derived by considering the
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}

inf-sup
condition (
6.3
), the Ritz-Stokes definition (
5.39
) and perturbation estimate (
5.26
), to see that

\displaystyle||\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{a({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-a_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})+a_{h}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathbf{v}_{h})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

Using the
L^{2}\times L^{2}

inf-sup
condition (
6.2
) instead, and the worse
H^{1}
coercivity bound
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{-1}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}
(
5.11
), we obtain, similarly, the following
L^{2}
bound for
\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})
:

\displaystyle||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\leq c(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{k_{g}-1})||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

Regarding the
H^{1}
bound (
5.47
), this follows from (
5.50
) and the
H^{1}
coercivity bound (
5.24
)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\mathbf{u}_{h}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\mathbf{u}_{h}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{a_{h}}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

Collecting the above results concludes the estimates in (
5.45
), (
5.46
) and (
5.47
).

To bound the tangential
L^{2}
-norm of the velocity we need to use a duality argument. We follow
[
17
, Theorem 6.14]
, so let us consider

({\mathbf{w}},\pi,\mu)\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}(\Gamma)
that satisfy

(5.52)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a({\mathbf{w}},{\mathbf{v}})\ +\!\!\!\!&b^{L}({\mathbf{v}},\{\pi,\mu\})=({\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}})),{\mathbf{v}})\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }{\mathbf{v}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma),\\
&b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\sigma,\xi\})=0\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{\sigma,\xi\}\in L_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}(\Gamma),\end{cases}

where we see that
{\mathbf{w}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
. Now, the surface
\Gamma
is sufficiently smooth and thus due to
[
39
, Lemma 2.1]
the solution also satisfies the following higher regularity estimate

(5.53)

||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}+||\{\pi,\mu\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}.

Set
{\mathbf{e}}_{h}={\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}
. Then, testing with
{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}={\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}
, we clearly see that

(5.54)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}=a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}})+b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\}).

We need to bound the two terms on the right-hand side of (
5.54
) appropriately. Let us start with the first term.
We see that

(5.55)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}})=a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))-a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})).

Let us bound each term of (
5.55
) separately. As mentioned before, we have
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
, therefore we are going to make use of the previously proven estimates (
5.45
)-(
5.47
), that is,
||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}}
.

•

By standard interpolation estimates (
4.25
) and norm equivalence (
4.19
) we first see that

(5.56)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{w}}-{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

•

We also see from the perturbation bound (
5.26
) that

(5.57)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))-a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

•

It remains to bound the last term in (
5.55
). Using the definition of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
) we have the following

(5.58)

\displaystyle a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))

\displaystyle=a_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))-a({\mathbf{u}},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))+b_{h}^{L}(\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}),\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{a_{h}({\mathbf{u}},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}})-a({\mathbf{u}},{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}})}_{\eqref{eq: Geometric perturbations a UNS}\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}}+\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\underbrace{a_{h}({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{w}})-a({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{w}})}_{\eqref{eq: Geometric perturbations a tangent extra regularity UNS}\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}}

\displaystyle+b_{h}^{L}(\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})+b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\}).

Now using once again the standard interpolation estimates, the bound of
b_{h}^{L}
(
5.16
) and the previously proven estimates (
5.45
), (
5.46
) we obtain

(5.59)

b_{h}^{L}(\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-{\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\leq c(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}+h^{r_{u}+2})||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

Furthermore, since
b^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=0
by (
5.52
), using the estimates (
5.45
), (
5.46
), the perturbation bounds (
5.28
), and considering
\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and
{\mathbf{r}}_{h}=\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}})-\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
we have

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})=b_{h}({\mathbf{w}},\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))-b({\mathbf{w}},\mathcal{P}^{\ell}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))+({\mathbf{w}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h},\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(({\mathbf{w}}-\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h},\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+({\mathbf{r}}_{h}\cdot({\mathbf{n}}_{h}-{\mathbf{n}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+({\mathbf{r}}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}},\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{k_{g}+\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{k_{g}+1+r_{u}})||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}+ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{r}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+(\underbrace{\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}({\mathbf{r}}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})}_{\in V_{h}=\Lambda_{h}},\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+\underbrace{({\mathbf{r}}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}-\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}({\mathbf{r}}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})}_{\leq ch||{\mathbf{r}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\text{ by }\eqref{eq: super-approximation estimate 2 UNS}},\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Def.}\eqref{eq: H^-1h definition UNS}}{\leq}\!\!\!\!\!ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}+ch||{\mathbf{r}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+c||{\mathbf{r}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Now taking the infimum over all
\widetilde{{\mathbf{w}}}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and since
{\mathbf{w}}
the solution to (
5.52
), from
[
17
, Lemma 6.12]
we have that
\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}}||{\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}||{\mathbf{w}}^{-\ell}-\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}+ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq ch||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}
. Therefore it is easy to check, by the Scott-Zhang interpolant estimate (
4.27
) and a simple triangle inequality, that

\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}}||{\mathbf{r}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}
. Plugging this result and the estimates (
5.45
), (
5.46
) to the previous bound, we arrive at

(5.60)

b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{w}},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)},

and therefore going back to (
5.58
) we obtain

(5.61)

\displaystyle a_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{w}}))\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

Inserting (
5.56
), (
5.57
) and (
5.61
) into (
5.55
) we finally obtain the bound for the first term:

(5.62)

\displaystyle a({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},{\mathbf{w}})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{w}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}.

We still need to bound
b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\})
in (
5.54
). Adding and subtracting suitable terms we get

(5.63)

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\})

\displaystyle=b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi),\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)\})+b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{{I}_{h}(\pi),{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle+b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\}).

Let us, once again, bound its term in (
5.63
) separately:

•

By the boundedness of the bilinear form
b^{L}
, standard interpolation estimates, and the norm equivalence (
4.19
) we get

(5.64)

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi),\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||\pi-{I}_{h}(\pi)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+c||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mu-{I}_{h}(\mu)||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}).

•

Using the perturbation estimate (
5.28
) we obtain

(5.65)

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{{I}_{h}(\pi),{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{e}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)})

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}).

•

For the last term in (
5.63
), first remember that
{\mathbf{u}}
satisfies (
1.1
). Then, using the definition of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
), the perturbation estimate (
5.29
) and standard interpolation estimates we obtain

(5.66)

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})

\displaystyle=b_{h}^{L}({\mathbf{u}},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}(\pi),\widetilde{I}_{h}(\mu)\})-\underbrace{b^{L}({\mathbf{u}},\{{I}_{h}(\pi),{I}_{h}(\mu)\})}_{=0}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}).

Combining (
5.64
),(
5.65
),(
5.66
) into (
5.63
) and remembering that
k_{g}\geq 1
we obtain

(5.67)

\displaystyle b^{L}({\mathbf{e}}_{h}^{\ell},\{\pi,\mu\})\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}(||\pi||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\mu||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}).

So, finally, inserting (
5.62
), (
5.67
) into (
5.54
) and remembering the regularity (
5.53
) the tangential
L^{2}
estimate (
5.44
) follows. The normal
L^{2}
estimate in (
5.44
) is calculated similarly to
[
17
, Lemma 6.16]
; we omit further details for the sake of brevity.

With similar arguments, one can prove the bounds in (
5.43
) and (
5.44
) for the arbitrary choice of
k_{\lambda}
(i.e.
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
) with
k_{g}\geq 2
. More precisely, instead of (
5.50
), and (
5.51
) we consider the error estimates in
[
17
, Theorem 6.6, Theorem 6.7]
and the worse
H^{1}
coercivity bound (
5.11
), therefore, we replace
\widehat{r}_{u}
by
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
. Using the
L^{2}\times L^{2}
discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition (
6.2
) instead, we get the pressure estimate (
5.43
). For the tangential
L^{2}
velocity estimate (
5.44
) we, again, need to apply a duality argument as before; it follows similarly to
[
17
, Theorem 6.7]
. For the sake of brevity, we do not provide any further details since we would just repeat the calculations.

Finally, the convergence for the time derivative
(\alpha=1)
is obtained in a similar way after taking the time derivative of the definition of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
), and considering the derivative of (
5.49
) with new
{\mathbf{g}}=\partial_{t}({\bm{f}}-\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}-\nabla_{\Gamma}p-\lambda{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}})
.
∎

Lemma 5.12

(Error Bounds Ritz-Stokes)
.

Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)\times(H^{1}(\Gamma)\times L^{2}(\Gamma))
the solution of (
3.6
). Then, we have the following error bounds for the Ritz-Stokes projection for
a=0,1

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{a_{h}}+||\partial_{t}^{a}\{p-\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}),\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}})\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{m}\big(||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}

(5.68)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \ \ \,+||\partial_{t}^{a}p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\partial_{t}^{a}\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}\big)

(5.69)

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b,\ell}({\mathbf{u}}))||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq ch^{m+1}\big(||\partial_{t}^{a}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\partial_{t}^{a}p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\partial_{t}^{a}\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}\big).

with
m=min\{r_{u},k_{pr}+1,k_{\lambda}+1\}
and
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
,
k_{g}\geq 2
, for
h\leq h_{0}
with sufficiently small
h_{0}
and with
c
independent of
h
.

Proof.

The proof follows similar to the proof of
Lemma

5.11
, where one considers
\mathbf{u}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
\{p_{h},\lambda_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
the Galerkin approximation of (
5.49
) with
{\mathbf{g}}={\bm{f}}-\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}
instead. Therefore since
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
the solution to (
3.6
),
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
is also the solution of (
5.49
). Then, by
[
17
, Theorem 6.6, Theorem 6.7]
we readily see, with the help of known consistency errors
Lemma

5.7
, that the following error estimates hold,

(5.70)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+||\{p-p_{h},\lambda-\lambda_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{m}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}+||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}\big),

(5.71)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{m+1}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}+||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}\big),

where
m=min\{r_{u},k_{pr}+1,k_{\lambda}+1\}
and
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
with
k_{g}\geq 2
.
Now the results (
5.12
) and (
5.69
) can be obtained analogously to
Lemma

5.11
and
[
17
, Section 6.3]
. For the sake of brevity we skip the calculations.
∎

Lemma 5.13

(
L^{\infty}
bound Ritz-Stokes projection)
.

Given
{\mathbf{u}}\in(W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma))^{3}
and
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
or
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
with
k_{g}\geq 2
, there exists a positive constant
c
independent of
h
such that the following estimate holds

(5.72)

||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}.

Proof.

Considering the
L^{\infty}
-bound (
4.26
) and the Ritz-Stokes estimates in
Lemmas

5.11
and
5.12
we see that for sufficiently small mesh-parameter
h
,

(5.73)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{-1}\big(||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{(\cdot)}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})-{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big)+||\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}+||\{p,\lambda\}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}.

Similarly, a bound for the gradient of the Ritz-Stokes projection can be established; see also
[
31
]
.
∎

5.5.
Discrete surface Leray projection

For
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
we define the discrete surface Leray projection
\Pi^{div}_{h}:L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})^{3}\rightarrow{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
to be the
L^{2}-
projection to the space of discrete weakly tangential divergence-free tangential fields
{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
(
5.2
), that is

(5.74)

\displaystyle(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}=({\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\qquad\forall\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}.

By definition, the projection is
L^{2}
-norm stable. If we want to achieve
H^{1}
-stability we need sufficiently smooth
\Gamma
and
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{V}}^{div}
.
Results of this type were shown in
[
9
]
in the case of quasi-uniform exact domain meshes. We extend them in the case of discrete surface
\Gamma_{h}
.

Lemma 5.14

(Leray Projection)
.

Let
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{V}}^{div}
, and assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, then there exists a constant
c>0
such that

(5.75)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+h||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}}\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)},

with
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
. The following stability result then also holds

(5.76)

||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}\leq||{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}}\qquad\forall{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{V}}^{div}.

Proof.

Considering the modified Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
) we see that
\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and therefore

||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}=\underbrace{(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell},\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}}_{=0\ (\text{by}\eqref{eq: discrete Leray UNS})}+(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell},\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

thus,
||\Pi^{div,\ell}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
. Using the Ritz-Stokes projection estimate (
5.45
) and the fact that
(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})={\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})+{\mathbf{n}}\otimes{\mathbf{n}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})
we obtain, for the tangential
L^{2}
norm,

(5.77)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}=({\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}),\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+({\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}),{\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{((\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{n}},(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}))\cdot{\mathbf{n}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}}_{\mathbf{(I)}}+ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

We need to bound the first term above. Setting
\mathbf{w}_{h}=\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, using (
5.22
), (
5.23
) when appropriate and recalling the geometric estimate
||{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}}\leq ch^{k_{g}}
; see (
4.5
), we obtain

(5.78)

\displaystyle\mathbf{(I)}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big(||{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+h^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big)

\displaystyle+||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+ch^{2k_{g}}||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+c||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big(h||{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+h^{k_{g}+1}||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big)\qquad\text{ (by \eqref{eq: divfree L2 norm kl=ku 2 UNS})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{2k_{g}}||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+ch^{k_{g}+1}||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+

\displaystyle+ch||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big(||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big),

where we also used the fact that
||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
. Now substituting (
5.78
) into (
5.77
) and applying the Ritz-Stokes estimates (
5.45
), (
5.48
) along with the fact that
2k_{g}\geq k_{g}+1\geq 2
for
k_{g}\geq 1
we get

(5.79)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq ch^{2\widehat{r}_{u}+2}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Then a simple application of Young’s inequality and a kickback argument gives our tangential
L^{2}
norm estimate in (
5.75
). Now, for the energy estimate in (
5.75
) we use the velocity normal approximation (
5.22
), and the previous tangential
L^{2}
error estimate to obtain the following

\displaystyle||\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}}+ch^{-1}||\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell}||_{a_{h}}+ch^{-1}\big(||{\mathbf{P}}(\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+ch^{k_{g}}||\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\ +||(\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}})\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\big)

(5.80)

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{k_{g}-1+\widehat{r}_{u}})||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)},

which gives us our desired result. Then the stability bound (
5.76
) follows from the previous result, and the Ritz-Stokes stability (
5.41
), since we can write the Leray projection as
\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}=(\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}})+\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}
.
∎

5.6.
The variational formulation

With the help of the bilinear forms and the skew-symmetrized trilinear form introduced in (
5.4
)-(
5.7
) we define the discrete variational formulation:

The linearized
unsteady fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
finite element method, which we analyze, is the following:

(UWL):
Given appropriate approximation of the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in V_{h}
, data
{\bm{f}}_{h}
, find
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
for
1\leq n\leq N
, such that

(UWL)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})\!\!&=({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\\
\phantom{aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa}b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\!\!&=0,\end{cases}

for all
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times\{Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}\}
, where we have linearized the skew-symmetrized inertia term, according to (
5.1
) and
Section

4.1
. For this linearized formulation it is easy to see that a solution exists and that it is also unique. More precisely, in
Section

6
we stablish stability estimates for the
velocity

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
in appropriate norms. However, it is not possible to find stability estimates for the two
pressures

\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}
. To do so, we need
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
to be uniformly bounded for each
n=1,...,N
in the energy
(a_{h})
-norm; see (
5.81
).

For that reason, we consider the following
auxiliary unsteady fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
method:

(UWL
b
):
Given appropriate approximation of the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, data
{\bm{f}}_{h}
, find
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in V_{h}
,
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
for
1\leq n\leq N
, such that,

(UWL
b
)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})\!\!&=({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\\
\phantom{aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa}b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\!\!&=0,\end{cases}

for all
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times\{Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}\}
.

Notice that the problem (
UWLb
), is the same as (
UWL
) when
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}
in the inertia term
\tilde{c}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
. Here
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}
is a vector-valued function which we, as mentioned above, assume is uniformly bounded in the following sense:

(5.81)

\sup_{n}||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq C_{b}.

This
assumption
helps us prove stability for the two pressures in the following
Section

6
(in reality just when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
; for arbitrary
k_{\lambda}
see
Remark

6.1
), which otherwise would not be possible, due to the skew-symmetrized form of
\tilde{c}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
and the use of
H^{1}-
conforming Taylor-Hood finite elements.

This
assumption
, however, is not all that unreasonable or strong. In particular, we are able to prove that solutions of the initial approximation of the surface Navier-Stokes problem (
UWL
), i.e. when
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}
, actually possess this property, in a later Section cf.
Remark

7.2
, once we prove velocity error estimates, assuming some low regularity assumptions are made for the solutions of the continuous problem (
1.1
).

More specifically, with the help of the Ritz-Stokes projection
Definition

5.2
, the convergence analysis, and low regularity assumptions for the solutions of the continuous surface Navier-Stokes system (
1.1
), we prove that (
5.81
) actually also holds for our discrete solution
{\mathbf{u}}_{h}^{n}
of (
UWL
). Nonetheless, we prove the ensuing
pressure stability
results with the help of eq. (
UWLb
) assuming that (
5.81
) holds.

Regarding the approximation of the initial data, we see that we have chosen that
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
. Eventually, we will see that we will want to choose
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
e.g.
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
or
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
. This means that the initial condition is then still
weakly tangential divergence-free
at the time step
t_{0}=0
, thus the stability of the velocity is unconditionally stable, see also
Remark

7.1
.

6.
Stability analysis

In this section, we derive stability results for our finite element schemes (
UWL
) and (
UWLb
). Specifically for the pressure stability we only consider (
UWLb
) assuming (
5.81
) holds. In this situation we also focus on the case where
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
in which just low regularity conditions for continuous velocity are needed; see
Remark

7.2
. In
Remark

6.1
we show how the pressure stability can also be established for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
. Nevertheless, let us start with an energy estimate involving the discrete velocity
\mathbf{u}_{h}
.

Lemma 6.1

(Velocity Estimate)
.

For the velocity solution
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
k=0,1,...,n
of (
UWL
) or (
UWLb
) we have the following energy estimates, which holds for
n\geq 1

(6.1)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq exp(ct_{n})\big(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=0}^{n}||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{k}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\big),

where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
an appropriate approximation
{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
, i.e.
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
.

Proof.

Testing the first equation of (
UWL
) or (
UWLb
) with
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
and the second one with
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}=\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}
, adding the two statements, remembering the skew-symmetry of
\tilde{c}_{h}(\bullet\,;\bullet,\bullet)
and using the algebraic identity
(a-b)a=\frac{1}{2}(a^{2}-b^{2})+\frac{1}{2}(a-b)^{2}
we get

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}-||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2})+\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}.

Now, summing the inequalities for
k=1,2,...,n
, applying general Young’s inequality and a standard kickback argument for small enough time step
\Delta t
we obtain

(6.3)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n}||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{k}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta t\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2},

where a standard discrete Gronwall’s inequality gives the desired result.
∎

We now present the two discrete inf-sup conditions, which play an important role in the pressure stability of our numerical scheme. These were proved in the case of the Lagrangian formulation of the surface Stokes problem in
[
17
, Lemma 5.6, Lemma 5.8]
.

Lemma 6.2

(
L^{2}\times L^{2}
Discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition)
.

Assume a quasi-uniform triangulation of
\Gamma_{h}
, then there exists a constant
\beta>0
independent of the mesh parameter
h
such that

(6.4)

\beta||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}}\ \ \ \forall\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}.

Lemma 6.3

(
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}
Discrete Lagrange
inf-sup
condition)
.

Assume a quasi-uniform triangulation of
\Gamma_{h}
, then there exists a constant
\beta>0
independent of the mesh parameter
h
such that

(6.5)

\beta||\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}}\ \ \ \forall\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}.

Now, as we mentioned in
Section

5.6
, let us consider (
UWLb
) assuming (
5.81
) holds.
To complete the stability results, we need to estimate the pressures
\{p_{h},\,\lambda_{h}\}
. But before we do so, we require to define an inverse operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}:\,{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}\rightarrow{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
. Given
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}

we may define the discrete inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}
as the unique solution to the discrete Stokes problem

(6.6)

\displaystyle a_{h}(\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})

\displaystyle=0

for every
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
. It is clear that the operator is coercive and also bounded, thus, it is well-defined. We can also see by testing appropriately that

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{\mathcal{A}_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle=||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{2}=(\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{w}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

\displaystyle||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{\mathcal{A}_{h}}

\displaystyle=||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

With the help of the above inverse Stokes operator we are able to show the auxiliary result:

Lemma 6.4

(Auxiliary bound)
.

Let assumption (
5.81
) hold. Then for the solution
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
of (
UWLb
) we have the following energy estimates, which holds for
n\geq 1

(6.7)

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Delta t||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq C({\mathbf{b}}_{h}){\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h}),

where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
an appropriate approximation,
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}:=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
,
{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h})
the bound in (
6.1
) and
C(\bullet)
a constant depending on the bound of
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}
(
5.81
).

Proof.

Testing the first equation of (
UWLb
) with
\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, see (
6.6
) where
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, and considering our choice of the initial value
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
we get for
n\geq 1
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle=({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Then we have the following estimates

•

By the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{h}
(
6.6
) we see that

(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}=\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}

•

Young’s inequality provides us with the estimate

\displaystyle a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

•

Recalling the assumption (
5.81
), the bound (
5.17
), and using Young’s inequality once again

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))

\displaystyle\leq||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c({\mathbf{b}}_{h})\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

•

Similar calculations also give

({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

Combining the above results to (
6.8
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq c\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+c({\mathbf{b}}_{h})\Delta t||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2},

where summing the inequality for
n=1,2,...,\,k
and
Lemma

6.1
yields our result.
∎

Before proving the pressure stability we need to bound some variant of the dual energy norm. Estimates for similar quantities have been proven in
[
10
]
for the Oseen problem,
[
2
,
29
]
for the Navier-Stokes problem in the
H^{-1}
-norm on fixed domains without variational crimes involving the domain approximation.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, then for a vector-valued function
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
the following estimate holds for constant
c>0
independent of time
t
, mesh-parameter
h

(6.9)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}}\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+c||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Proof of
Lemma

6.5
is given in
Appendix

B
. We are now able to finalize the stability results concerning the two pressures.
We prove
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure bounds from which we can easily see that the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{H_{h}^{-1}}
stability estimate also follows.

Lemma 6.6

(
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
Pressure Bounds)
.

Let
Lemma

6.2
and the assumptions in
Lemma

6.4
and
Lemma

6.5
hold. Then, for the pressure solutions
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}\in Q_{h}\times L_{h}
,
k=1,...,n
of (
UWLb
) the following stability estimate holds true

(6.10)

\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq C({\mathbf{b}}_{h}){\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}={\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0},{\bm{f}}_{h})
the bound in (
6.1
) and
C(\bullet)
a constant depending on the bound of
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}
(
5.81
).

Proof.

Recall the discrete
L^{2}\times L^{2}

inf-sup
condition in
Lemma

6.2
, we have

(6.11)

\displaystyle||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}},

where due to (
UWLb
), and the bounds of our bilinear and trilinear forms in
Lemma

5.3
we get

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})

\displaystyle=-(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})+({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})

\displaystyle\leq|(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}|+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Therefore, going back to (
6.11
), by
Lemma

6.5
we have

(6.12)

\displaystyle||\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}+c(1+||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}})||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where we used the definition of the discrete inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}
(
6.6
) to see that

\displaystyle||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{-1}||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

hence
||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{-1}||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}
. Squaring and multiplying (
6.12
) by
\Delta t
we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta t||\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq c\Delta t||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\Delta t(1+||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2})||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\Delta t||{\bm{f}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}.

Finally, summation for
k=1,...,n
and using the bounds (
6.1
), (
6.7
) along with the assumption (
5.81
) regarding the uniform bound of
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}
results in our desired estimate.
∎

Remark 6.1

(About
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability for arbitrary
k_{\lambda}
)
.

Notice in
Lemma

6.6
we had to assume
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
due to
Lemma

6.5
, since it does not hold
for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
; see
Remark

7.5
for further details. To prove pressure stability in the
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
case we need further assumptions for
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}
, mainly that

(6.13)

\sup_{n}||{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq C_{b}.

Then we can establish
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability bounds, by testing the main equation with
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}
and finding a stability estimate for

\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{n}||\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

instead of the inverse Stokes operator, similar to how we handle the error estimates in
Section

7.3
for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
. We omit further details, as the calculations are straightforward. We will also see that this new assumption (
6.13
) holds only if further regularity assumptions are imposed; see
Remark

7.3
for further details.

7.
Error Analysis

For the error analysis, we consider the linearized
unsteady fully discrete Lagrangian surface Navier-Stokes
finite element method (
UWL
), that is, we choose
{\mathbf{b}}_{n}^{n-1}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}
for (
UWLb
). Let us introduce the problem (
UWL
) once more for clarity reasons:

Find
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
,
\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
such that,

(UWL)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\frac{\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\})\!\!&=({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}),\\
\phantom{aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa}b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\!\!&=0,\end{cases}

for all
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times\{Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}\}
, with the approximation of the source term
{\bm{f}}_{h}^{\ell}={\bm{f}}
, and where the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}
is an appropriate approximation of
{\mathbf{u}}^{0}={\mathbf{u}}(t_{0})={\mathbf{u}}(0)
. In our case, as we mentioned before, we suppose that
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
. This initial condition means that even at time-step
t_{0}=0
our solution is still
discrete weakly tangential divergence-free
. Now we will consider
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
, the modified Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
). In
Section

7.3
we will see that we choose the initial condition as
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
. Depending on the choice of initial condition we arrive at a different discrete scheme.

Remark 7.1
.

Different approximations could have been selected as the initial condition, yet each choice would present different difficulties. For example

-

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\widetilde{I}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0})
: In this case
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}
is not weakly divergence-free at time-step 0, then we can only have condition stability, and an inverse type CFL condition is necessary, see also
[
6
]
.

-

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
: The main issue is that in practice the initial pressures will be unknown.

For convenience we, once again, omit writing the inverse lift extension
(\cdot)^{-\ell}
since it should be clear from the context whether a quantity is defined on
\Gamma
or
\Gamma_{h}
, e.g. for functions
{\mathbf{u}},\,p,\,\lambda
.

The error analysis follows typical arguments. Let us define the errors and decompose them into an interpolation error and a discrete error in the appropriate finite element spaces. For that we consider the modified Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
) for the velocity
{\mathbf{u}}
and normal Lagrange interpolant for the two pressures
\{p,\lambda\}
:

(7.1)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}={\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{({\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n})}_{\text{Interpolation error}}+\underbrace{(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}+\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},

(7.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{p}^{n}=p^{n}-p_{h}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(p^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n})}_{\text{Interpolation error}}+\underbrace{(\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n}-p_{h})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{p}^{n}+\theta_{p}^{n},

(7.3)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{n}=\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\lambda^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n})}_{\text{Interpolation error}}+\underbrace{(\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}-\lambda_{h})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\rho_{\lambda}^{n}+\theta_{\lambda}^{n}.

The bounds of the modified Ritz-Stokes interpolation error have already been proved; see
Lemma

5.11
.
Note also that now
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
for all
n=0,1,...,N
, due to the choice of interpolant and the choice of the initial condition. The interpolation errors for the pressures are well-known

(7.4)

\displaystyle||p^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq h^{k_{pr}+1}||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}},

\displaystyle||\lambda^{n}-\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq h^{k_{\lambda}+1}||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}}.

7.1.
Consistency and velocity a-priori estimates

Assumptions 1

(Regularity assumptions I)
.

We assume that the solution
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
has the following regularity

\displaystyle\{p,\lambda\}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma))\times L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{u}}\in H^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap W^{1,\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap H^{1}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))\cap L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)).

We remind the weak formulation (
3.6
):

(7.5)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+a({\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\ +c({\mathbf{u}};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\ +\!\!\!\!&b^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell},\{p,\lambda\})=({\bm{f}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\in V_{h}^{\ell},\\
&b^{L}({\mathbf{u}},\{q_{h}^{\ell},\xi_{h}^{\ell}\})=0\ \ \ \text{ for all }\{q_{h}^{\ell},\xi_{h}^{\ell}\}\in Q_{h}^{\ell}\times\Lambda_{h}^{\ell},\end{cases}

for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. For the error analysis we consider the pair
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
as the strong solution of (
1.1
) therefore we may rewrite the inertia from now on as

(7.6)

\displaystyle c({\mathbf{u}};{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\,d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}\,d\sigma\Big),

where we have used integration by parts formula (
2.12
), and the fact that
{\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
and
\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}{\mathbf{u}}=0
.

We start by trying to find an error equation that involve the discrete remainder
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}
with respect to some consistency, interpolations and inertia errors. As mentioned before, by the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
)
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and therefore we are able to decouple the pressure discrete remainders from the error equations, which is crucial to find error bounds just for
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}
independent of
\theta_{p},\,\theta_{\lambda}
. Thus, we present the subsequent Lemma as follows:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times(Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h})
be test functions, then for
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}},\,\theta_{p},\theta_{\lambda}
as in (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
) the following error equation holds true for
n\geq 1

(7.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\frac{\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\,+\!\!\!\!\!&b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})=\sum_{i=1}^{5}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}),\\
&b_{h}^{L}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=0,\end{cases}

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
,
with consistency errors:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

•

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})-b^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})

•

\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

•

\textsc{Err}_{4}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=({\bm{f}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

•

\textsc{Err}_{5}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

and interpolations errors:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=-(\frac{\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

•

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=-b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\rho_{p}^{n},\rho_{\lambda}^{n}\})

•

\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

Proof.

Recall the decompositions (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
). Then, using our finite element approximation (
UWL
) the following calculations hold

\displaystyle D_{n}:=(\frac{\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})=-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+(\frac{\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n},\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}\})+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}).

Recalling the continuous equation (
7.5
) at time
t=t^{n}
we get

\displaystyle D^{n}

\displaystyle=-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+(\frac{\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle+a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n},\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}\})

\displaystyle=({\bm{f}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\frac{\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle+a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\widetilde{I}_{h}p^{n},\widetilde{I}_{h}\lambda^{n}\})-b^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=-(\frac{\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\rho_{p}^{n},\rho_{\lambda}^{n}\})-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle+(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})-b^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell},\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\})

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})+({\bm{f}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-a({\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=:\text{Err}_{1}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\text{Err}_{2}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle+\text{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\text{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\text{Err}_{3}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\text{Err}_{4}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\text{Err}_{5}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}),

where in the second to last equality we used the fact that

(7.8)

\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n};\mathbf{v}_{h})=-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}).

Finally, by the modified Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
), we can clearly see that
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
for all
n\geq 0
, thus the second equation in (
7.7
) follows.
∎

Before deriving the error estimates, let us establish appropriate bounds for the consistency and interpolation errors. For the interpolation errors we can prove the following estimates.

Lemma 7.2

(Interpolation errors)
.

Let

\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{2}(I_{n};(H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))^{3}),

then the following interpolation bounds hold

(7.9)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}^{I}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{\Delta t}}||\partial_{t}\rho_{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

(7.10)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}^{I}_{2}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c(h^{k_{pr}+1}+h^{k_{\lambda}+1})(||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

with constant
c>0
independent of
h
. If furthermore
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, then the following holds

(7.11)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}^{I}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}+1}}{\sqrt{\Delta t}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
and constant
c>0
independent of
h
.

Proof.

Let us start with the interpolation error
\textsc{Err}^{I}_{1}(\cdot)
. We notice that the first term may be rewritten as

\displaystyle(\frac{\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\partial_{t}\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}(t,\cdot)\,dt||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Then, using the fact that

\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\partial_{t}\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}(t,\cdot)dt\leq\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n})},

coupled with the estimate of the time derivative of the modified Ritz-Stokes interpolation operator (
5.43
), we obtain our desired result, i.e.

\displaystyle(\frac{\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{\Delta t}}||\partial_{t}\rho_{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Next, (
7.10
) can be calculated with the help of the continuity bound (
5.16
) and interpolation errors (
7.4
).

Now, regarding (
7.11
) we follow the same footsteps as above but this time we split it as followed

\displaystyle(\frac{\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle=(\frac{{\mathbf{P}}(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})}{\Delta t},{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\frac{(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})\cdot{\mathbf{n}}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot({\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{n}}_{h}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+(\frac{(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})\cdot{\mathbf{n}}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{\Delta t}}\big(||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{P}}\rho_{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}+ch||\partial_{t}\rho_{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where we applied the geometric error (
4.6
), and the normal bound (
5.22
), since
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
. Recalling the modified Ritz-Stokes estimates (
5.48
) and (
5.45
) our desired result follows.
∎

Lemma 7.3

(Inertia error)
.

Given that
{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};(H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))^{3}),
the following bounds are satisfied, for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}

(7.12)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(7.13)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c\big(||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

In the case where
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}
, considering the skew-symmetric nature of the inertia term, we arrive at the following

(7.14)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|

\displaystyle\leq c(||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}})||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}.

Proof.

Let us start with (
7.12
). Remember that
\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})=-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})
. Considering (
5.1
), the fact that
||\bm{H}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}}\leq c
and the continuity bound (
5.17
) of the trilinear form, we see that

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\leq||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Similarly, with the additional use of the stability estimates for the discrete velocity (
6.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2},

proving the first bound (
7.12
).

By employing the decomposition (
7.1
), we can also derive the following

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{h}(\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\tilde{c}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\big(||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}+c||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

In a similar manner, for the second term, by applying the stability estimates in
Lemma

6.1
, and once more utilizing the bound (
5.17
), we compute that

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})+\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\rho_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+c||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Combining the two results, we get the second bound (
7.13
). Finally, (
7.14
) inequality becomes apparent when one considers the skew-symmetric nature of the inertia term as expressed by (
5.1
) and
Remark

5.1
. ∎

Lemma 7.4

(Consistency errors)
.

Assume

\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{2}(I_{n};(L^{2}(\Gamma))^{3}),\quad\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};(L^{2}(\Gamma))^{3})\cap L^{2}(I_{n};(L^{4}(\Gamma))^{3}),

then we have the following consistency bounds

(7.15)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}+h^{k_{g}+1}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

(7.16)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}(||p^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+||\lambda^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(7.17)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}+c\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

(7.18)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{4}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\bm{f}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, where constant
c>0
independent of
h
.

Proof.

Let us begin with the first consistency error, involving the time derivative.

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}:
By means of changing the domain of integration, see (
4.21
) for
{\bm{f}}=\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}
, and integration by parts

\displaystyle(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}=(\frac{\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}}{\mu_{h}},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle+(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\frac{\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)-{\mathbf{u}}_{t}^{n}}{\Delta t}\,dt,\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\frac{1}{\Delta t}(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}(t-t_{n-1})\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)\,dt,\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+\frac{1}{\Delta t}(\Big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}(t-t_{n-1})^{2}\,dt\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}(t,\cdot)^{2}\,dt\Big)^{1/2},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\Delta t)^{1/2}||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where in the last two inequalities we used the norm equivalence (
4.19
) and Schwarz inequality.

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}
,
\textsc{Err}_{4}^{C}:
These estimates are an immediate consequence of (
5.7
) and (
4.21
) respectively.

\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C}:
We split this error into two parts: one associated with geometric errors and another concerning the different time steps in the first argument. So, adding and subtracting appropriate terms yields

(7.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle+c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}).

For the first line on the right-hand side of (
7.19
), by (
5.1
) (see also
Remark

5.1
) and (
7.6
), along with the fact that
\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}=\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}({\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{u}})+({\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})\bm{H}
, we see that it is equal to

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}.

Using the bounds in
Lemma

4.4
and the geometric estimates
||{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{k_{g}}
,
||{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathbf{P}_{h}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{k_{g}}
,
|1-\frac{1}{\mu_{h}}|\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}
as seen in (
4.5
), (
4.6
), (
4.8
) respectively, along with the fact that
{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}={\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot({\mathbf{n}}_{h}-{\mathbf{n}})
, we clearly see that

(7.20)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\big)

\displaystyle+\Big|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot({\mathbf{n}}_{h}-{\mathbf{n}}){\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\Big|+\Big|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}){\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}){\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}

where in the last inequality we used (
5.31
) with
\beta=\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}
, and
\mathbf{v}_{h}={\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{h})
. For the second and last lines of (
7.19
), we have from (
7.6
) that

\displaystyle|c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma}_{\textbf{I}c}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma}_{\textbf{II}c}.

We focus on
(\textbf{II}c)
, since
(\textbf{I}c)
will follow in a similar way. Using the fact that

\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t^{n}}\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n})},

a straightforward calculation reveals that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\textbf{II}c=\int_{\Gamma}(\Big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t^{n}}\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)dt\Big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\leq\sqrt{\Delta t}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{4}(\Gamma)},

where we made use of
||\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq c||{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}||_{a}\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}
by the perturbation bound (
4.24
), the
H^{1}
coercivity bound (
5.13
). Similarly, recalling the bound (
5.17
) we get

(7.22)

\textbf{I}c\leq\sqrt{\Delta t}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}.

To complete the proof, we insert (
7.20
) and (
7.21
), (
7.22
) into (
7.19
) and recall the embedding
L^{4}\hookrightarrow H^{1}
.
∎

Now we are ready to show the error estimates involving the velocity. We split it into two cases. One where we assume
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
and a second one where we assume
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
. Let us start with the first case.

Lemma 7.5
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, and that the regularity
1
holds.
Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
), with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
. Then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N
:

(7.23)

\displaystyle||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}
