\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
, with
C=C(u,p,\lambda,{\bm{f}})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\bm{f}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}.

Proof.

We test (
7.7
) with
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}=\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\}
and subtract to obtain

(7.24)

\displaystyle||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\Delta t||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\Delta t\Big(|\sum_{i=1}^{4}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|+|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|+|\mathcal{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|\Big),

where
\textsc{Err}_{5}^{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})=0
by the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.2
).
Now we calculate the terms in the last line according to the interpolation and consistency estimates found previously. From
Lemma

7.4
and
||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}
, cf. (
5.24
), using Young’s inequality appropriately we see that

(7.25)

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{4}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|

\displaystyle\leq c\Delta t\big(||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}^{2}\big)+ch^{2k_{g}}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||p^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+ch^{2k_{g}+2}||{\bm{f}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

For the interpolation errors due to
Lemma

7.2
and specifically (
7.11
) we, likewise, have

(7.26)

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|\leq c\frac{h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}+2}}{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}+c(h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2})\big(||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{5}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

Lastly, by employing the bound (
7.14
) in
Lemma

7.3
for the inertia term, the Ritz-Stokes projection estimate (
5.45
) and two Young’s inequalities we obtain

(7.27)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n})|\leq ch^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}(1+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}})||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+c||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

Combining (
7.25
), (
7.26
), and (
7.27
) into (
7.24
), after a kickback argument we get

(7.28)

\displaystyle||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\frac{4}{5}\Delta t||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq(1+c\Delta t)||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\frac{2}{5}\Delta t||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle+c\Delta t\Big(\Delta t\big(||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}^{2}\big)+\frac{ch^{2\widehat{r}_{u}+1}}{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle+ch^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}(1+||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}})||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+c(h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2})\big(||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+ch^{2k_{g}+2}||{\bm{f}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\Big),

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
. Now, since
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}=0
, due to the choice of the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
, and since
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
by (
5.2
), upon summation over
k=1,...,n
of (
7.28
) and straightforward application of the discrete Gronwall’s inequality, it yields

(7.29)

\displaystyle||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq cexp(ct_{n})\Big(h^{2k_{g}+2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}||{\bm{f}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta t^{2}\big(||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];L^{4}(\Gamma))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+Ch^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}\big(||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)\big)

\displaystyle+(h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2})\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)\Big),

where the constant
C
on the third line emerged as a result of applying the discrete stability estimate
\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}\leq C
; see (
6.1
). This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
,
k_{g}\geq 2
, and that the regularity
1
holds.
Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
), with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
. Then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N
:

(7.30)

\displaystyle||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
, with
C=C(u,p,\lambda,{\bm{f}})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\bm{f}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}.

Proof.

The proof follows as in
Lemma

7.5
, where instead of the Ritz-Stokes estimate (
5.45
) (for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
) we use the worse estimate (
5.43
) throughout. Moreover, in (
7.26
) we use the bound (
7.9
) instead of (
7.11
), and in (
7.25
) we use instead
||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq h^{-1}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}
cf. (
5.11
). We omit further details.
∎

Remark 7.2

(About
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability)
.

Now that we have tracked the regularity needed (
1
) and proved the estimates (
7.23
) and (
7.30
), we are able to go back and prove a bound of the form (
5.81
) for
{\mathbf{b}}_{h}^{n-1}=\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}
. Using the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.39
), we notice that

(7.31)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}+h^{-1}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where in the last inequality we have used the inverse inequality. Now it is easy to see, due to the stability of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.41
) and convergence results (
7.23
) (or (
7.30
)), that the assumption (
5.81
) holds, more precisely we have that

(7.32)

\displaystyle\sup_{n}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\leq C,

if the assumptions in
Lemma

7.5
(or
Lemma

7.6
) hold and
\Delta t\leq ch.

This time-step condition is reasonable since it will be automatically satisfied if we try to balance spatial and temporal discretization errors, as this leads to
\Delta t\sim h^{k_{u}}
.

Remark 7.3

(About
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
)
.

As mentioned before in
Remark

6.1
, to prove
L^{2}_{L^{2}}\times L^{2}_{L^{2}}
pressure stability for arbitrary choice of
k_{\lambda}
, we would need to prove
\sup_{n}||{\mathbf{u}}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c
, cf. (
6.13
). To see this, first recall the
L^{\infty}
-bound of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.73
), then using an inverse inequality we see that

(7.33)

\displaystyle||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}+h^{-1}||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

which yields our desired result considering the velocity error convergence estimates (
7.23
) or (
7.30
) and assuming
\Delta t\leq ch
, cf.
Remark

7.2
, along with further regularity for the velocity
{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma))
.

Theorem 7.7

(Velocity Error Estimates)
.

Under the Assumption
1
and
Lemma

7.5
the following velocity error estimates hold for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.34)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k}-{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
, with
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.5
.

Furthermore, under the Assumption
1
and
Lemma

7.6
we instead have the following velocity error estimates hold for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.35)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k}-{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
, with
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.6
.

Proof.

This can be readily seen from the decomposition (
7.1
), the triangle inequality, the interpolation estimates (
5.43
), (
5.45
) and the estimates of the discrete remainder in (
7.23
), (
7.30
).
∎

7.2.
Pressure a-priori estimates for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}

We now consider
1
and the time-step condition in
Remark

7.2
, that is

\Delta t\leq ch.

We also recall that (
7.32
) holds under the assumptions in
Lemma

7.5
. We now establish a-priori estimates for the two pressures when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
. We will make use of the ideas introduced in the stability analysis section (
Section

6
), that is, the discrete inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}
(
6.6
) to bound the approximation of the time derivative in an appropriate negative norm,
and the
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}

inf-sup
condition (
6.5
) to establish optimal pressure a-priori estimates.

Lemma 7.8

(Auxiliary result)
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
, and that the regularity
1
holds, with time-step
\Delta t\leq ch
. Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
), with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
. Recalling the bound (
7.32
), then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.36)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Delta t||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq C\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
and
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.5

Proof.

We start by testing our error equations (
7.7
) with
\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
(which is rigorous since
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
for all
n\geq 1
), then recalling (
6.6
) we derive that

(7.37)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle+a_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))\leq 2\Big(|\sum_{i=1}^{4}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|

\displaystyle+|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|+|\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|\Big),

where we also utilized that
\textsc{Err}_{5}^{C}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))=0
, since
\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
. Now we want to bound the rest of the results appropriately. Again, this is similar to
Lemma

6.4
. The idea is a suitable use of Young’s inequality followed by a kickback argument against the first term. We have the following:

•

The second can be bounded as

\displaystyle a_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))\leq c\Delta t||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

•

For the interpolations errors using
Lemma

7.2
and specifically (
7.11
) we get

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{I}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|\leq\Delta t\frac{h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}+2}}{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle+c\Delta t(h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2})\big(||p^{n}||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda^{n}||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

•

For the consistency errors using
Lemma

7.4
and
||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}
, cf. (
5.24
), we may obtain

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{4}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|\leq c(\Delta t)^{2}\big(||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+c\Delta th^{2k_{g}}\big(||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||p^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\lambda^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+c\Delta th^{2k_{g}+2}||{\bm{f}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

•

Finally we can bound the inertia term with the help of the uniform estimate (
7.32
) and bound (
7.12
) in
Lemma

7.3

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}))|\leq c\Delta t||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}+C\Delta t||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{5\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2},

where
C
is the constant in (
7.32
).

Now putting everything together, using a kickback argument against the term
\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})||_{a_{h}}^{2}
, summing for
k=1,...,n
and using the convergence results involving the velocity,
Lemma

7.5
and (
7.34
) in
Theorem

7.7
, we get our desired result (
7.36
).
∎

We now want to finish the convergence results by providing error estimates for the two pressures. First, it suffices to control the discrete remainders
\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\}
. For that, we will use the same techniques as in pressure stability estimates; see
Lemma

6.6
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
and that the regularity
1
hold, with time-step
\Delta t\leq ch
. Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let

\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
), with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
. Recalling the bound (
7.32
), then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.38)

\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{\theta_{p}^{k},\theta_{\lambda}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq C\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
\widehat{r}_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
,
and
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.5
.

Proof.

Recall the
L^{2}\times H_{h}^{-1}

inf-sup
condition
Lemma

6.3
, then since
\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
it holds

(7.39)

\displaystyle||\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}}.

Considering now the error equation (
7.7
), solving for
b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})
, using the Ritz-Stokes projection
Definition

5.2
, and the velocity error estimate (
7.34
) in
Theorem

7.7
, along with the bounds (
7.9
)–(
7.10
) in
Lemma

7.2
, (
7.12
) in
Lemma

7.3
, and (
7.15
)–(
7.18
) in
Lemma

7.4
, we have the following

(7.40)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\theta_{p}^{n},\theta_{\lambda}^{n}\})

\displaystyle\leq|(\frac{\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}|+||\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+C\Big(\frac{c}{\sqrt{\Delta t}}||\partial_{t}\rho_{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+c(h^{k_{pr}+1}+h^{k_{\lambda}+1})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+ch^{k_{g}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\Big)+\Big(\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{4}(\Gamma))}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\Big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+\big|b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\big|

Now we mainly need to bound the first and last term. For the first term, similar to
Lemma

6.6
, since
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, with the use of (
6.9
) and the definition of the discrete inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}
(
6.6
) we have that

(7.41)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{|(\frac{\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}|}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}}\leq c||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{h}(\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})}{\Delta t}||_{a_{h}}.

For the last term, the bound (
5.16
), the super approximation estimates (
4.30
), and (
4.31
), the modified Ritz-Stokes interpolation estimates (
5.45
), (
5.46
) and the
H_{h}^{-1}
-norm definition (
5.3
),
yield

(7.42)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big|b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}),\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\})\big|\leq||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+\Big|\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot({\mathbf{n}}_{h}-{\mathbf{n}})\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})}_{||{\mathbf{n}}_{h}-{\mathbf{n}}||_{L^{\infty}}\leq ch^{k_{g}}\text{ by }\eqref{eq: geometric errors 1 UNS}}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})+\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\big(\widetilde{I}_{h}^{SZ}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})-\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}\big)\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathcal{P}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+ch||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathcal{L}_{h}({\mathbf{u}})||_{H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{\widehat{r}_{u}}||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Now we substitute (
7.40
)-(
7.42
) into (
7.39
), and factor out the term
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}
(since
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}\leq||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}
). Then, squaring and applying the operator
\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}
, along with the velocity estimates (
7.23
) and the auxiliary convergence result (
7.36
), we obtain our final result.
∎

Now, by the decompositions (
7.2
), (
7.3
), the interpolation bounds (
7.4
), and a simple application of the triangles inequality one may then derive the following estimate for the error in the pressures.

Theorem 7.10

(Pressures Error Estimate)
.

Under the Assumptions
1
and
Lemma

7.9
the following velocity error estimates holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.43)

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{{\mathbf{e}}_{p}^{k},{\mathbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})\times H_{h}^{-1}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq C\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2\widehat{r}_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}\}
with
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.5
.

7.3.
Pressure a-priori estimates for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}

Now we want to prove a-priori estimates when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
. Let us briefly discuss the differences between this case and
Section

7.2
.

First, as we saw in
Remark

7.3
and will also observe from the a-priori pressure estimates later on, we require
further regularity assumptions
. Second, since we cannot follow the steps in
Section

7.2
because
Lemma

6.5
does not hold for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
(see
Remark

7.5
for further details where one may prove a similar estimate in that case) instead of calculating an estimate for the inverse Stokes operator as in
Lemma

7.8
, we prove an error bound for the approximation of the time derivative in the following norm
\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{n}||\frac{\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}.

As we shall see, such estimate is not immediately attainable by testing the error equation (
7.7
) with
\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\theta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}
, since terms involving test function in the energy norm
||\cdot||_{a_{h}}
, see for example (
7.10
), (
7.16
), (
7.17
) and (
7.13
), would give suboptimal order of convergence.

For that reason, we seek bounds for the consistency, interpolation and inertia errors such that the test functions are controlled by the
L^{2}
-norm instead. To achieve this, first, we need to consider the standard Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.40
), this time, thus removing the first two equations mentioned above (
7.10
), (
7.16
) involving the bilinear forms
b^{L},\,b_{h}^{L}
.
We will also consider new initial condition for our discrete scheme (
UWL
);
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
. This is so that at time
t_{0}=0
the
discrete remainder
in (
7.44
)
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}=0
; see
Remark

7.1
. Second, for the inertia terms we will have to apply integration by parts (
5.25
), (
2.12
). All these changes, compared to
Section

7.2
, come at the expense of higher regularity assumptions, which are the following:

Assumptions 2

(Regularity assumptions II)
.

We assume that the solution
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
has the following regularity

\displaystyle\{p,\lambda\}\in W^{1,\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma))\times W^{1,\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{u}}\in H^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap W^{1,\infty}([0,T];W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma))\cap L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma))\cap W^{1,\infty}([0,T];H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)),

Considering the standard Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.12
) we use the following new decompositions:

(7.44)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}={\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{({\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}))}_{\text{Interpolation error}}+\underbrace{(\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}:=\eta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}+\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},

\displaystyle\{{\mathbf{e}}_{p}^{n},{\mathbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{n}\}=\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\}-\{p_{h}^{n},\lambda_{h}^{n}\}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\{p^{n},\lambda^{n}\}-\{\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}),\mathcal{L}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})\}}_{\text{Interpolation error}}+\underbrace{(\{\mathcal{P}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}),\mathcal{L}_{h}^{b}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})\}-\{p_{h},\lambda_{h}\})}_{\text{discrete remainder}}

(7.45)

\displaystyle:=\eta_{\{p,\lambda\}}^{n}+\sigma_{\{p,\lambda\}}^{n}.

The bounds on the Ritz-Stokes interpolation error have already been proved in
Lemma

5.12
. Due to the new pressure decompositions (
7.3
), and the new initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
, the new error equation is the following.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
(\mathbf{v}_{h},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}\times(Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h})
be test functions, then for
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}},\,\sigma_{\{p,\lambda\}}
as in (
7.44
), (
7.3
) the following error equations hold true
n\geq 1

(7.46)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\frac{\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+a_{h}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})\,+\!\!\!\!\!\!&b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h},\sigma_{\{p,\lambda\}}^{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h})+\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}),\\
&b_{h}^{L}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n},\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\})=0,\end{cases}

for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}\in Q_{h}\times\Lambda_{h}
, with the consistency errors:

with consistency errors:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}^{n}-{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}-(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}

•

\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

•

\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=({\bm{f}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}-({\bm{f}}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

and interpolations errors:

•

\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=-(\frac{\eta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\eta_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}}{\Delta t},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

•

\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h}):=-\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

Proof.

The proof follows exactly as in
Lemma

7.1
, where we utilize the Ritz-Stokes projection as described in (
5.40
). This time, due to (
7.3
), the consistency and interpolation errors related to the bilinear form
b_{h}^{L}(\cdot,\{\cdot,\cdot\})
cancel out, leaving us with the remaining terms unchanged.
∎

We can readily see from
Section

7.1
, and specifically following similar calculations to
Lemma

7.6
and
Theorem

7.7
that the following error estimates for the velocity hold:

(7.47)

\displaystyle||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}\big),

(7.48)

\displaystyle||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{k}||_{a_{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cexp(ct_{n})\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big),

where for the last estimate we used the standard Ritz-Stokes interpolation estimates (
5.12
).

Remark 7.4
.

Let us discuss, once more, the initial condition and the condition (
7.33
):

i)

Regarding the initial condition, we chose
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
such that
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}=0
and therefore the computations become straightforward at
t=0
. For example, the previous estimate (
7.47
) is immediate from
Section

7.1
or in the later auxiliary result
Lemma

7.15
no
a_{h}
-norm of
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}
appears after a summation. In practice, though, this is not convenient as mentioned in
Remark

7.1
. Therefore a more practical choice would have been
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}
and since, by
Definitions

5.2
and
5.3
, the Ritz-Stokes projection is linear, it would then give that
||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}||_{a_{h}}=||\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}-\mathcal{R}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}||_{a_{h}}=||\mathcal{R}_{h}(0,\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})||_{a_{h}}\leq c(h^{k_{pr}+1}+h^{k_{\lambda}+1}+h^{k_{g}})(||p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}}+||\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}})
, and so our convergence results would still be optimal.

ii)

By the
L^{\infty}
stability of the standard Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.73
) and (
7.47
)

Remark

7.3
still stands.

Now before moving forward, as mentioned in the beginning of the subsection, we need to re-calculate our consistency, interpolation and inertia bounds. From
Lemma

7.2
and Ritz-Stokes estimate (
5.12
) we have the following.

Lemma 7.12

(Interpolation errors II)
.

Let

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};(H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma))^{3}),\,\partial_{t}p\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)),\,\partial_{t}\lambda\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)),

then the following interpolation bound holds

(7.49)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}^{I}_{1}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{r_{u}}\sup_{t\in I_{n}}\big(||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

(7.50)

\displaystyle+c(h^{k_{pr}+1}+h^{k_{\lambda}+1})\sup_{t\in I_{n}}\big(||\partial_{t}p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}+||\partial_{t}\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
and constant
c>0
independent of
h
.

Lemma 7.13

(Consistency errors II)
.

Assume

\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{2}(I_{n};(L^{2}(\Gamma))^{3}),\quad\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};W^{1,{\infty}}(\Gamma)^{3}),

then we have the following consistency bounds

(7.51)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{1}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c\big(\sqrt{\Delta t}||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}(I_{n};L^{2}(\Gamma))}+h^{k_{g}+1}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

(7.52)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{2}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c\big(h^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+\Delta t||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\sup_{t\in I_{n}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{1,{\infty}}(\Gamma)}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

(7.53)

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{3}^{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}||{\bm{f}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

for
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, where constant
c
independent of
h
.

Proof.

The first and third consistency terms are bounded as the consistency errors in
Lemma

7.4
. For the second estimate, as in
Lemma

7.4
, we split the error into two parts: one associated with the integration domain errors and another concerning the different time steps of the first argument in the inertia term:

(7.54)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle+c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}),

then (
7.20
) and the inverse inequality yield

(7.55)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Moving onto the second line of (
7.54
), deferring from
Lemma

7.4
we have that

(7.56)

\displaystyle|c({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})-c({\mathbf{u}}^{n};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma}_{\textbf{I}c}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma+\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma}_{\textbf{II}c}.

We can bound the initial line on the right-hand side as:

(7.57)

\displaystyle\textbf{I}c=\int_{\Gamma}(\Big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t^{n}}\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot)dt\Big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma\leq\Delta t\sup_{t\in I_{n}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

For the second estimate
\textbf{II}c
we perform a simple integration by parts (
2.12
) so that we may bound the test function w.r.t. the
L^{2}
-norm. Let us consider the first integral of
\textbf{II}c
, since the second follows similarly. Recalling that
{\mathbf{u}}^{n}
is tangent as the solution of the continuous problem (
3.6
), we obtain

(7.58)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot(\nabla_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}))\;d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\;d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma}(({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma.

So considering (
7.58
) it is clear that

(7.59)

\displaystyle\textbf{II}c=\textbf{I}c+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}^{n})(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\;d\sigma-\int_{\Gamma}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}^{n-1})(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\;d\sigma}_{\textbf{III}c},

where now, as before, we are able to bound the last term as followed

(7.60)

\displaystyle\textbf{III}c=\int_{\Gamma}(\Big(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t^{n}}\partial_{t}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}(t,\cdot))dt\Big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\;d\sigma\leq\Delta t\sup_{t\in I_{n}}||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{1,{\infty}}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Combining (
7.60
) into (
7.59
), considering the bound (
7.57
) and plugging everything into (
7.56
) we get our desired estimate.
∎

Lemma 7.14

(Inertia error II)
.

Given that

{\mathbf{u}}\in L^{\infty}(I_{n};(W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma))^{3}),

it follows that the following bound holds true:

(7.61)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+c||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+c||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

where
c>0
constant independent of
h
.

Proof.

Recall that

(7.62)

\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{v}_{h})=-\underbrace{\tilde{c}_{h}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})}_{\textbf{C}_{1}}-\underbrace{\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})}_{\textbf{C}_{2}}.

Starting with the first term on the right-hand side, we have

(7.63)

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h}){\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big).

The last two term are easily bounded by

(7.64)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

with the help of the
L^{\infty}
bounds of the Weingarten map
\bm{H}_{h}
i.e.
||\bm{H}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c
; see (
4.5
). Due to our assumed regularity the first integral on the first line of (
7.63
) is bounded by

(7.65)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}\leq||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

where we have also used the norm equivalence (
4.19
). For the second integral in (
7.63
), we want our test function in the
L^{2}
-norm, as in (
7.65
). For that, recall first that
||{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathbf{P}_{h}}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{k_{g}}
, cf. (
4.5
), and the inverse inequality
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{-1}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
, then we readily see that

(7.66)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}=\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{P}_{h}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{k_{g}-1}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}.

Now the integration by parts formula (
5.25
) yields

(7.67)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{v}_{h})\cdot{\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}=\int_{\Gamma_{h}}{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1})(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\,d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}){\mathbf{u}}^{n})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}

\displaystyle+\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}\int_{T}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{n}}_{h})({\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{h})({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\,d\sigma_{h}+\sum_{E\in\mathcal{E}_{h}}\int_{E}[{\mathbf{m}}_{h}]\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\,d\ell.

Therefore, using the co-normal bound (
4.5
),
||\cdot||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{E}_{h})}\leq h^{-1/2}||\cdot||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
(see proof of
Lemma

5.3
) and the fact that
k_{g}\geq 2
we obtain

(7.68)

\displaystyle\big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}})\mathbf{v}_{h}){\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big|\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

So combining (
7.64
)-(
7.68
) yields that (
7.63
) is bounded by

(7.69)

\textbf{C}_{1}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

We still have to bound
\textbf{C}_{2}
. We are going to apply an analogous idea to the one used to bound the previous estimate. But first let us rewrite it in the following way

(7.70)

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{2}=\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})=-\tilde{c}_{h}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})-\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}),

where
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}
as in the first decomposition (
7.44
) and
\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}
the standard Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.40
). Now, the first term is bounded, thanks to (
5.17
), as followed

(7.71)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq c||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

The second term, by (
5.1
) and
Remark

5.1
is equal to

(7.72)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{h}(\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1};{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}}((\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}((\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big).

Due to the
L^{\infty}-
bound of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.72
) we have

(7.73)

\displaystyle\big|\int_{\Gamma_{h}}({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h})\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\bm{H}_{h}{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

(7.74)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}}((\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n})\mathbf{v}_{h}\;d\sigma_{h}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

It remains to bound the second in (
7.72
). This follows exactly as in the previous calculations (
7.66
) - (
7.68
), where now using the
W^{1,\infty}-
bound of the Ritz-Stokes projection (
5.72
) we readily see that

(7.75)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}}((\mathcal{R}^{b}_{h}{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov})\mathbf{v}_{h}){\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}\;d\sigma_{h}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

Combining now (
7.71
)-(
7.75
) yields that (
7.70
) is bounded by

(7.76)

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+c||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

To obtain the final result we simply merge the two estimates
\textbf{C}_{1}
(
7.69
) and
\textbf{C}_{2}
(
7.76
) into (
7.62
).
∎

In contrast to
Lemma

7.8
we now prove the following stronger result, without the help of an inverse Stokes operator.

Lemma 7.15

(Auxiliary result II)
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1},\,k_{g}\geq 2
and that the regularity
2
hold with time-step
\Delta t\leq ch
. Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
) with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0},\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
. Then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.77)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Delta t||\frac{(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1})}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq C_{2}\big(1+C\frac{h^{2r_{u}}}{\Delta t}\big)\big((\Delta t)^{2}

\displaystyle+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2(k_{pr}+1)}+h^{2(k_{\lambda}+1)}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}
with positive constant
C_{2}=C_{2}(u,p,\lambda,{\bm{f}})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(||\partial_{t}p||_{H^{k_{pr}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}\lambda||_{H^{k_{\lambda}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{k_{u}+1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||{\bm{f}}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+||{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma)}^{2}+||\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}||_{W^{1,{\infty}}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)+||\partial_{tt}{\mathbf{u}}||_{L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}
and constant
C
as in
Lemma

7.6
.

Proof.

To start, we test the error equation (
7.46
) with
\mathbf{v}_{h}=\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}
, and
\{q_{h},\xi_{h}\}=\sigma_{\{p,\lambda\}}^{n}
, thus we derive for
1\leq n\leq N
, since
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}=0
due to the choice of the initial condition, that

(7.78)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}-||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq 2\Big(|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})|

\displaystyle+|\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})|+|\mathcal{C}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})|\Big).

We want to approximate the right-hand side appropriately. This will be possible with the help of the newly established bounds. A simple use of Young’s inequality gives us the following bounds:

•

Using
Lemma

7.12
the interpolation error is bounded as

\displaystyle|\textsc{Err}_{1}^{I}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})|\leq C_{2}\Delta t(h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2(k_{pr}+1)}+h^{2(k_{\lambda}+1)})+\frac{1}{4\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}.

•

For the consistency errors considering the results in
Lemma

7.13
we see that

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\textsc{Err}_{i}^{C}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})|\leq C_{2}\Delta t((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2k_{g}-2})+\frac{1}{4\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}.

•

Finally, using the inverse and Young’s inequality appropriately,
\mathcal{C}(\cdot)
can be bounded with the help of the estimates in
Lemma

7.14
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1})

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\Delta t\big(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{4\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+ch^{-2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\Delta t||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\Delta t\big(||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}.

Now combining all the above, applying the velocity error estimates in (
7.47
), (
7.48
) and employing a kickback argument for the two terms
\frac{1}{\Delta t}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}
and
||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}
and summing for
k=1,...,n
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Delta t||\frac{(\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1})}{\Delta t}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}

\displaystyle+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{k-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}\leq C_{2}\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2k_{pr}+2}+h^{2k_{\lambda}+2}\big)

(7.79)

\displaystyle+C\big(\frac{1}{\Delta t}+ch^{-2}\big)\big((\Delta t)^{2}+h^{2r_{u}}\big)\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}(||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{2}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}^{2}),

since
\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{0}=0
due to the choice of the initial condition, where
C
the constant in
Lemma

7.6
. Now, since
k_{u},\,k_{g}\geq 2
and
\Delta t\leq ch
, another application of (
7.47
), and (
7.48
) give our desired result.
∎

We now finish the convergence results by providing error estimates for the two pressures. First, as mentioned before, it suffices to control the discrete remainders
\{\sigma_{p}^{n},\sigma_{\lambda}^{n}\}
.

Lemma 7.16
.

Assume
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1},\,k_{g}\geq 2
and that the regularity
2
hold with time-step
\Delta t\leq ch
. Let
({\mathbf{u}},\{p,\lambda\})
be the solution of (
1.1
) and let
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{k}
and
\{p_{h}^{k},\lambda_{h}^{k}\}
,
k=1,...,n
be the discrete solutions of (
UWL
) with initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}^{b}{\mathbf{u}}^{0}=\mathcal{R}_{h}({\mathbf{u}}^{0}\{p^{0},\lambda^{0}\})
. Then the following estimate holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.80)

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{\sigma_{p}^{k},\sigma_{\lambda}^{k}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq C_{2}\big(1+C\frac{h^{2r_{u}}}{\Delta t}\big)\big((\Delta t)^{2}

\displaystyle+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2(k_{pr}+1)}+h^{2(k_{\lambda}+1)}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}

and constants
C_{2}
,
C
the same as in
Lemma

7.15
and
Lemma

7.6
respectively.

Proof.

The proof follows similarly to
Lemma

7.9
, but with the new auxiliary result
Lemma

7.15
. So, recalling the
L^{2}\times L^{2}

inf-sup
condition (
6.4
)

(7.81)

\displaystyle||\{\sigma_{p}^{n},\sigma_{\lambda}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}}\frac{b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\sigma_{p}^{n},\sigma_{\lambda}^{n}\})}{||\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n}||_{a_{h}}}.

and using the error equation (
7.46
) coupled with the estimates proved above we find

\displaystyle b_{h}^{L}(\mathbf{v}_{h}^{n},\{\sigma_{p}^{n},\sigma_{\lambda}^{n}\})

\displaystyle\leq||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}-\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle+C_{2}(\Delta t+h^{r_{u}}+h^{k_{pr}+1}+h^{k_{\lambda}+1})||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+C_{2}(||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}+||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}\big)||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle+c||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{1/2}||\sigma_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{n-1}||_{a_{h}}^{1/2}||{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{u}}^{n}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Substituting the inequality into (
7.81
) factoring out the test function
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}
, squaring and applying the operator
\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}
, it is clear then that the velocity error convergence results (
7.47
), and (
7.48
) and the afore-mentioned auxiliary estimate (
7.77
) yields us our final result.
∎

Finally, by applying the triangle inequality on the decomposition (
7.3
), the above
Lemma

7.16
and the Ritz-Stokes interpolation error (
5.12
) we obtain our final theorem.

Theorem 7.17

(Pressures Error Estimate II)
.

Under the Assumptions
2
and
Lemma

7.16
the following pressure error estimates holds for
1\leq n\leq N

(7.82)

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{n}||\{{\mathbf{e}}_{p}^{n},{\mathbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{n}\}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2}\leq C_{2}\big(1+C\frac{h^{2r_{u}}}{\Delta t}\big)\big((\Delta t)^{2}

\displaystyle+h^{2r_{u}}+h^{2(k_{pr}+1)}+h^{2(k_{\lambda}+1)}\big),

where
r_{u}=min\{k_{u},k_{g}-1\}

and constants
C_{2}
,
C
the same as in
Lemma

7.15
and
Lemma

7.6
respectively.

Remark 7.5

(Lower Regularity convergence estimate)
.

As mentioned before, to prove the pressure error estimates for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
we needed
2
instead of the lower regularity
1
. This happened because we could not follow the steps in
Section

7.2
and more particularly because
Lemma

6.5
does not hold in that case, which in turn means we cannot find an estimate for the inverse Stokes operator (
6.6
) in the energy
a_{h}
-norm. Let us explain in more detail why
Lemma

6.5
does not hold and how a change in the scheme (
UWL
) could enable us to find pressure error estimate for the low regularity
1
:

-

In the proof of
Lemma

6.5
in
Appendix

B
we see in (
B.3
) that we need an energy stability estimate for the Leray projection
\Pi_{h}^{div}
(
5.74
). When
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
this is not possible, since we cannot “control” the normal part appropriately, therefore only a bound of the form
||\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}
is feasible.

-

If one considers instead the leray projection
({\mathbf{P}}\Pi^{div}_{h}{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}=({\mathbf{u}}^{-\ell},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
, for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
then it is possible to prove
||{\mathbf{P}}\Pi_{h}^{div}{\mathbf{u}}||_{a_{h}}\leq c||{\mathbf{u}}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}
.

-

Going back to (
B.3
) it is then clear that by also defining a new inverse Stokes operator such that
a_{h}(\mathcal{A}_{h}^{P}\mathbf{u}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})=(\mathbf{u}_{h},{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
, for all
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
, we can find an estimate similar to (
6.9
) in
Lemma

6.5
, w.r.t. this new
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{P}
inverse Stokes operator instead.

-

Now, we need an auxiliary bound similar to (
6.7
) for
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{P}
. However, with our current scheme (
UWL
) that is not possible, since the first term of (
6.8
) cannot be controlled, unless we consider a tangential
L^{2}
-norm (due to the new inverse Stokes operator
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{P}
).

-

This means that in our discrete scheme (
UWL
) we have to consider the tangential
L^{2}
-norm for approximating the time derivative. Therefore, the ensuing results all follow (remember that the
a_{h}
-norm does include an
L^{2}
-norm) where we replace the bounds and error estimates for the
L^{2}
-norm, with the tangential
L^{2}
-norm, e.g.
||{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\sim||{\mathbf{P}_{h}}\mathbf{u}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
.

8.
Numerical results

We now present some numerical results to validate our theoretical conclusions. We start in
Section

8.1
with a general setup of our experiments, defining the parameters, the formulations used, and specifying the notation.
In
Section

8.2
we examine the choice of the two parameters
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
and
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
, and test our numerical results against the theoretical findings. Finally, in
Section

8.3
we compare our scheme (
UWL
), which utilizes an extra Lagrange multiplier (L.M.) to enforce the tangentiality condition to an extension of the penalty formulation (P.M.) for the unsteady surface Navier-Stokes; an appropriate penalty parameter is used; see
[
43
]
for the Stokes case.

8.1.
Setup

The numerical results were implemented using the
Firedrake
package
[
21
]
, where the linear systems were solved using GMRES with the help of LU preconditioners. The finite element approximations are carried out on sequences of mesh-refinements in order to calculate experimental orders of convergence.

Instead of using the approximation of the inertia term
\tilde{c}_{h}(\bullet;\bullet,\bullet)
introduced in (
5.1
), and
Remark

5.1
and used throughout the analysis, we use the following for our implementation

(8.1)

c_{h}(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1};\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n},\mathbf{v}_{h}):=\int_{\Gamma_{h}}((\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov})\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})\mathbf{v}_{h},

where we see no difference in convergence quality for all quantities. Using this approximation for the inertia term means that we eventually avoid any kind of approximation involving geometric quantities such as the Weingarten map, Gauss curvature, etc. We, finally, introduce the following notation for the error norms used

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}})^{2}

\displaystyle:=||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}({\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}_{h})||_{\ell^{2}(L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}^{2}=\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N}||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{p}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}})^{2}

\displaystyle:=||p-p_{h}||_{\ell^{2}({L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}))}}^{2}=\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N}||p^{n}-p_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0\leq n\leq N}||u^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},\qquad\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}:=\max_{0\leq n\leq N}||{\mathbf{P}_{h}}({\mathbf{u}}^{n}-\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0\leq n\leq N}||\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\cdot{\mathbf{n}}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}.

8.2.
Varying curvature surface

In this example we consider the closed and compact surface
\Gamma
in
[
15
, Example 4.8]
which is a the level set function

\phi(x)=\frac{1}{4}x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+\frac{4x_{3}^{2}}{(1+\frac{1}{2}\sin(\pi x_{1}))^{2}}-1\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

It is constructed by mapping from a discretised unit sphere
\mathcal{S}
with the help of the mapping

F(p)=(2p_{0},\,p_{1},\,\frac{1}{2}p_{2}(1+\frac{1}{2}\sin(2\pi p_{0})))\quad p\in\mathcal{S},

such that
\Gamma=F(\mathcal{S})
. We describe an exact solution of the unsteady surface Navier-Stokes problems (
1.1
), (
1.3
) as

(8.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}=\textbf{curl}_{\Gamma}\psi,

\displaystyle\text{with}\ \psi=(1-2t)\frac{1}{2\pi}\cos(2\pi x_{1})\cos(2\pi x_{2})\cos(2\pi x_{3}),

\displaystyle p=\sin(\pi x_{1})\sin(2\pi x_{2})\sin(2\pi x_{3}).

((a))

((b))

((c))

((d))

Figure 1
.
Varying Curvature Surface | Velocity
\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}
at different times for
k_{g}=3
,
k_{u}=2
,
k_{pr}=1
,
k_{\lambda}=1
, with mesh size
h=0.09
and
\Delta t=0.01
. The length and direction of the arrows depict the strength and orientation of the current.

From
[
42
]
we know that the surface curl is tangential. So, our solution is tangential and divergence-free, while
p\in L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)
. The data
{\bm{f}}
,
g
, then, on the right-hand side and the Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
can be calculated numerically with the help of the exact solution as interpolation of the smooth data. Obviously in our case
g=0
.

We compare the primitive solutions depending on the parameter choice
k_{\lambda}
. That is, the choice of the approximation space
\Lambda_{h}
of the extra Lagrange multiplier
\lambda_{h}
. We have our two standard
cases
: When
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}=2
, therefore
V_{h}=\Lambda_{h}
, and when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1=1
. So, we use
\mathrm{\mathbf{P}}_{2}
/
\mathrm{P}_{1}
/
\mathrm{P}_{2}
or
\mathrm{\mathbf{P}}_{2}
/
\mathrm{P}_{1}
/
\mathrm{P}_{1}

Taylor-Hood
finite elements. Specifically, for the
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
case, as seen in
Theorems

7.7
and
7.17
due to the limiting geometric convergence rate
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{k_{g}-1})
we need to use
super-parametric surface finite elements
, that is, the geometric (surface) approximation to be considered one order higher than the approximation order of the velocity F.E. space
{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
, e.g.
k_{g}=k_{u}+1=3
. On the other hand, we do not need this extra geometric approximation for
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
.

We set the viscosity parameter
\mu=1/2
and start with an initial mesh size of
h_{0}=0.66
and time-step
\Delta t_{0}=0.5
. Then, we consider a series of refinements such that the spatial refinement is halved while the temporal refinement is reduced by a factor of four, so we have
\Delta t\sim h^{2}
. That allows us to obtain optimal convergence; see
Theorems

7.7
,
7.10
and
7.17

Figure 2
.
Varying Curvature Surface | Velocity
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
norm
-
Errors
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
| For different choice of
k_{\lambda}

The convergence results are presented in
Fig.

2
and
Fig.

3
. Regarding the covariant derivative error (energy norm)
\mathcal{E}_{\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
, we observe, as expected from
Theorem

7.7
, a second order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2})
convergence for both
cases
(remember for
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
we use super-parametric finite elements
k_{g}=3
). The same can be said about the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
error of the pressure
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
, see
Theorems

7.10
and
7.17

About the tangential
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
-norm we observe higher than expected
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{3})
convergence for both
cases
(since
\Delta t\sim h^{2}
), which shows that the spatial error is dominant; see
Theorem

7.7
. This behavior, regarding the spatial error, aligns with previous findings on the optimal convergence of the tangential velocity in the Stokes case; see
[
17
, Section 6.4]
. In fact, due to the tangential Ritz-Stokes bounds (
5.48
) and (
5.44
) we should expect this type of convergence, despite not deriving such estimate. On the other hand, regarding the full
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
velocity error, we observe that the convergence rate for the
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}

case
(
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{3})
) , is one order higher
({\mathcal{O}}(h^{2}))
compared to the
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1

case
. This, again, aligns with previously proved estimates for the Stoke cases in
[
17
, Section 6.4]
.

Lastly, about the tangential approximation, determined by
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
, exhibits the expected characteristics of
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2})
order of convergence when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1
, while when
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
we observe a
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2.5})
convergence rate. This, once again, despite not deriving such estimate, exhibits similar behaviour to the Stokes case; see
[
17
, Theorem 6.16]
. This estimate can be proven with the help of the Ritz-Stokes estimates (
5.48
).

Figure 3
.
Varying Curvature Surface | Left: Velocity energy norm
-
Error

\mathcal{E}_{\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
. | Right: Pressure
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
norm
-
Error
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
. | For different choice of
k_{\lambda}

We see that the choice of
k_{\lambda}
only plays an important role in the optimal convergence and control of the normal component of the velocity, since the tangent part of our solution convergences optimally regardless of the choice of
k_{\lambda}
(depending, of course, on the geometric approximation as well). On the other hand, as noticed in
[
19
]
and in our experiments, setting
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
drastically worsens the condition of the underlying system’s equations as
h
approaches zero. So, there is an interplay between choosing
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}}
for optimal convergence (including the full
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
velocity) using
iso-parametric surface finite elements
while worsening the system’s condition and choosing
\underline{k_{\lambda}=k_{u}-1}
, where the condition of the system of equations is better, but one gets optimal convergence (only for tangent part of the solution) when using using
super-parametric surface finite elements
, i.e. higher approximation of the geometry.

8.3.
Example 2: Comparison

In this example we perform a simple comparison test for the Penalty method (
P.M
) and the Lagrange multiplier method (
L.M
).

We consider a unit sphere
\mathcal{S}
with the exact smooth solutions of (
1.3
)

(8.3)

{\mathbf{u}}=J(t){\mathbf{K}}_{z},\quad p=(1+t)^{3}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3},

where
J(t)=(1+x_{3}(2+0.5t)^{3})
and
{\mathbf{K}}_{z}
the Killing vector field around the z-axis (
x_{3}
in our case), with
||{\mathbf{K}}_{z}||=4
, thus it satisfies
E({\mathbf{K}}_{z})\equiv 0
, where
E
the rate-of-strain tensor. The pressure also satisfies
p\in L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)
. The right-hand side
{\bm{f}},\,g
and the Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
can then be calculated with the help of the exact solution above.

We consider a natural extension of the penalty formulation, as presented in
[
43
]
for the surface Stokes equations, in the case of the time-dependent surface Navier-Stokes equations. For this penalty formulation, we use
\mathrm{\mathbf{P}}_{2}-\mathrm{P}_{1}

Taylor-Hood
iso-parametric surface finite elements with surface order approximation
k_{g}=2
. In order to obtain optimal convergence we need to take care of the new penalty term introduced

\tau(\mathbf{u}_{h}^{n}\cdot\tilde{{\mathbf{n}}}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot\tilde{{\mathbf{n}}}_{h}).

More specifically, we need to choose a higher-order approximation for the normal
\tilde{{\mathbf{n}}}_{h}
, than the usual
{\mathbf{n}}_{h}
, i.e.
||{\mathbf{n}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{n}}}_{h}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq ch^{k_{g}+1}
, and also choose the penalty term appropriately. For the construction of such normal
\tilde{{\mathbf{n}}}_{h}
see
[
22
, Remark 3.3]
for further details. In several literature
[
22
,
40
,
37
]
it has also been shown that choosing
\tau=\alpha h^{-2}
leads to optimal convergence. In our example, we observed that choosing
\tau=2.5h^{-2}
produced optimal convergence.

Figure 4
.
Sphere | Velocity-pressure
L^{2}_{L^{2}}

:
\{k_{g}=2,k_{u}=2,k_{pr}=1,k_{p}=3\}
,
L.M.

\{k_{g}=3,k_{u}=2,k_{pr}=1,k_{\lambda}=1\}
.

For the Lagrange multiplier method (L.M.), we prefer to use
\mathrm{\mathbf{P}}_{2}/\mathrm{P}_{1}/\mathrm{P}_{1}

Taylor-Hood

super-parametric finite elements
, with surface order approximation
k_{g}=3
. See the above example in
Section

8.2
regarding the choice of approximation of the F.E. space
\Lambda_{h}
, i.e. the choice of
k_{\lambda}
.

In both cases, we set the viscosity parameter
\mu=1/2
. For both formulations of the problem, we also used a sufficiently accurate quadrature rule. We started with an initial mesh size of
h_{0}=0.62
and time-step
\Delta t_{0}=0.5
. We then consider a series of refinements such that the spatial refinement is halved while the temporal refinement is reduced by a factor of four, such that
\Delta t\sim h^{2}
. That, then, allows us to obtain optimal convergence; see
Theorems

7.7
and
7.17

.

|
P.M.
:
\{k_{g}=2,k_{u}=2,k_{pr}=1,k_{p}=3\}
,
L.M.
:
\{k_{g}=3,k_{u}=2,k_{pr}=1,k_{\lambda}=1\}
.

In
Fig.

4
we notice that the
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
errors in the full velocity differ for the two formulations. Specifically, for the Lagrange multiplier method
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
convergences with second order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2})
, while for the penalty method we have optimal convergence of third order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{3})
. On the other hand, we see that both the
L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}
errors for the tangential velocity agree. This agrees with
Theorem

7.7
and the results that involve the optimal convergence of the tangent part of the velocity, as seen in the Stokes case
[
17
]
, which should also be valid in our context of the time-dependent surface Navier-Stokes; see
Lemma

5.12
. Finally, we notice that both
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
errors of the pressure
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
exhibits second order convergence.

In
Fig.

5
, we also see that the
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
error of the covariant derivative
\mathcal{E}_{\nabla_{\Gamma}^{cov}{\mathbf{u}}}^{L^{2}_{L^{2}}}
for the two methods agree, again displaying optimal second-order convergence (
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2})
). Finally, we observe that the tangential condition is “better” enforced in the Penalty Method (P.M), More specifically, we see that the normal velocity error
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}}^{L^{\infty}_{L^{2}}}
converges with order
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{3})
when we enforce the tangential condition via the penalty term, and
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{2})
when we enforce it via the Lagrange multiplier. We get same order of convergence
{\mathcal{O}}(h^{3})
if we choose
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}=2
instead, for our choice of
Taylor-Hood
finite elements, as seen in our previous example
Section

8.2
.

Appendix A
Leray-Helmholtz decomposition

In this appendix we show the Leray-Helmholtz decomposition presented in Lemma
3.1
.

Lemma A.1

(Helmholtz-Leray decomposition)
.

For every
{\mathbf{u}}\in(H^{1}(\Gamma))^{3}
there exists unique
\phi\in H^{2}(\Gamma)
,
{\mathbf{u}}_{n}\in(H^{1}(\Gamma))^{3}
and
\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}^{div}
such that

{\mathbf{u}}=\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}})+{\mathbf{u}}_{n}+\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi.

Proof.

For
{\mathbf{u}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
, since
{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\in C^{3}(\Gamma)
we readily see that
{\mathbf{u}}_{n}=({\mathbf{u}}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}){\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=u_{n}{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
. Now we consider the following surface Laplace problem: Let
\phi\in H^{1}(\Gamma)\cap L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)
be solution to,

\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi=\int_{\Gamma}\xi\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n}),

for every
\xi\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
. Since
{\mathbf{u}}\in\bm{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
, we then see that
{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n}\in\bm{H}^{1}_{T}
and thus
\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n})\in L^{2}(\Gamma)
. But, we may also notice that

\int_{\Gamma}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n})=\int_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}_{T}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}){\bm{\kappa}}=0,

hence we also have that
\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n})\in L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)
. Therefore, we see that the solution is unique and well defined. With the help of the inverse Laplacian operator
\Delta_{\Gamma}^{-1}:H^{-1}(\Gamma)\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma)
we also see that it satisfies
\Delta_{\Gamma}^{-1}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n})=\phi
. The elliptic regularity theorem
[
15
]
shows that
||\nabla_{\Gamma}(\Delta_{\Gamma}^{-1}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n}))||_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq||\phi||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq c||\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}({\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n})||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}
. Setting now

\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}})={\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{u}}_{n}-\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi

we clearly see that
\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}}))=0
and
\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}})\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
, and and therefore
\Pi^{div}({\mathbf{u}})\in{\mathbf{V}}^{div}
. Finally, the uniqueness follows directly from the uniqueness of the solution of the surface Laplace equation.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of
Lemma

6.5

Proof of
Lemma

6.5
.

Lifting from
\Gamma_{h}
to
\Gamma
, using the decomposition in
Lemma

A.1
since
\mathbf{v}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}
and
\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
, and going back to
\Gamma_{h}
, we see that

(B.1)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}=(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\mathbf{w}_{h},(\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi)^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

where
\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}=(\Pi^{div}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell})^{-\ell}
,
{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}^{-\ell}
, and
\phi\in H^{2}(\Gamma)
such that
||\phi||_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq||\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq c||\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}
, where the last inequality holds due to the perturbation bound (
4.23
) and the fact that
||\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c||\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}^{cov}\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}
.

We bound its term of (
B.1
) appropriately. Let us start backwards. The third term is bounded by

(B.2)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h},(\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi)^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{w}_{h},(\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi)^{-\ell}-\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\mathbf{w}_{h},\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{w}_{h},(\nabla_{\Gamma}\phi)^{-\ell}-\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi^{-\ell})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+(\mathbf{w}_{h},\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(\phi^{-\ell}-\widetilde{I}_{h}(\phi^{-\ell})))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle\leq ch||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

where we used the fact that
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
and
\widetilde{I}_{h}(\phi^{-\ell})\in S_{h,k_{g}}^{k_{pr}}
the standard Lagrange interpolant (In fact
\widetilde{I}_{h}(\phi^{-\ell})\notin L^{2}_{0}(\Gamma_{h})
but see that we take gradient of this interpolant), and the geometric bound (
4.22
).

For the second term, recall again that
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
with
k_{\lambda}=k_{u}
, therefore we use (
5.18
) and geometric error (
4.6
) to obtain

(B.3)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}},\mathbf{v}_{h}\cdot{\mathbf{n}})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c(h+h^{k_{g}})||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}

We are left with the first term. It follows by the discrete Leray-projection (
5.74
) that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}=(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}+\underbrace{(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}-\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}}_{:=0},

and since
\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}
such that
\Pi^{div}\mathbf{v}_{h}^{\ell}\cdot{\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}}=0
and the discrete Leray projection
\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h})\in{\mathbf{V}}_{h}^{div}
we are able to use the inverse Stoke operator (
6.6
) to show that

(B.4)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}))_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}

\displaystyle=a_{h}(\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h}))

\displaystyle\leq c_{d}||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\Pi_{h}^{div}(\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h})||_{a_{h}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{d}||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}},

where we used the Leray projection stability bound (
5.76
) in the last inequality.
Therefore we see that

(B.5)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{w}_{h},\Pi^{div,-\ell}\mathbf{v}_{h})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq c_{d}||\mathcal{A}_{h}\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{a_{h}}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}+c_{d}h||\mathbf{w}_{h}||_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h})}||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}.

Combining (
B.2
)-(
B.5
) along with the fact that
||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h})}\leq||\mathbf{v}_{h}||_{a_{h}}
gives us our desired result.
∎
